A counter-example to Singer’s conjecture for the algebraic transfer
Nguyễn Sum

Abstract
. Write P k := 𝔽 2 ​ [ x 1 , x 2 , … , x k ] P_{k}:=\mathbb{F}_{2}[x_{1},x_{2},\ldots,x_{k}] for the polynomial algebra over the prime field 𝔽 2 \mathbb{F}_{2} with two elements, in k k generators x 1 , x 2 , … , x k x_{1},x_{2},\ldots,x_{k} , each of degree 1. The polynomial algebra P k P_{k} is considered as a module over the mod-2 Steenrod algebra, 𝒜 \mathcal{A} . Let G ​ L k GL_{k} be the general linear group over the field 𝔽 2 \mathbb{F}_{2} . This group acts naturally on P k P_{k} by matrix substitution. Since the two actions of 𝒜 \mathcal{A} and G ​ L k GL_{k} upon P k P_{k} commute with each other, there is an inherit action of G ​ L k GL_{k} on 𝔽 2 ⊗ 𝒜 P k \mathbb{F}_{2}{\otimes}_{\mathcal{A}}P_{k} . Denote by ( 𝔽 2 ⊗ 𝒜 P k ) n G ​ L k (\mathbb{F}_{2}{\otimes}_{\mathcal{A}}P_{k})_{n}^{GL_{k}} the subspace of 𝔽 2 ⊗ 𝒜 P k \mathbb{F}_{2}{\otimes}_{\mathcal{A}}P_{k} consisting of all the G ​ L k GL_{k} -invariant classes of degree n n . In 1989, Singer [ 25 ] defined the homological algebraic transfer φ k : Tor k , n + k 𝒜 ​ ( 𝔽 2 , 𝔽 2 ) ⟶ ( 𝔽 2 ⊗ 𝒜 P k ) n G ​ L k , \varphi_{k}:\mbox{Tor}^{\mathcal{A}}_{k,n+k}(\mathbb{F}_{2},\mathbb{F}_{2})\longrightarrow(\mathbb{F}_{2}{\otimes}_{\mathcal{A}}P_{k})_{n}^{GL_{k}}, where Tor k , k + n 𝒜 ​ ( 𝔽 2 , 𝔽 2 ) \mbox{Tor}^{\mathcal{A}}_{k,k+n}(\mathbb{F}_{2},\mathbb{F}_{2}) is the dual of Ext ( 𝔽 2 , 𝔽 2 ) k , k + n 𝒜 {}_{\mathcal{A}}^{k,k+n}(\mathbb{F}_{2},\mathbb{F}_{2}) , the E 2 E_{2} term of the Adams spectral sequence of spheres. In general, the transfer φ k \varphi_{k} is not a monomorphism and Singer made a conjecture that φ k \varphi_{k} is an epimorphism for any k ⩾ 0 k\geqslant 0 . The conjecture is studied by many authors. It is true for k ⩽ 3 k\leqslant 3 but unknown for k ⩾ 4 k\geqslant 4 . In this paper, by using a technique of the Peterson hit problem we prove that Singer’s conjecture is not true for k = 5 k=5 and the internal degree n = 108 n=108 .

footnotetext:

Mathematics Subject Classification
. Primary 55S10; 55S05, 55T15.

Keywords and phrases:
Steenrod squares, Polynomial algebra, Singer algebraic transfer, modular representation.

1.
Introduction

Denote by
BS_{k}
the classifying space of an elementary abelian 2-group
S_{k}
of rank
k
. It is well-known that the cohomology algebra
P_{k}:=H^{*}(BS_{k})
is the polynomial algebra
\mathbb{F}_{2}[x_{1},x_{2},\ldots,x_{k}]
in
k
variables
x_{1},x_{2},\ldots,x_{k}
, each of degree 1. Here the cohomology of the topological space
BS_{k}
is taken with coefficients in the field
\mathbb{F}_{2}
with two elements.

Then, the algebra
P_{k}
is considered as a module over the mod-2 Steenrod algebra,
\mathcal{A}
. The action of
\mathcal{A}
on
P_{k}
is explicitly determined from the elementary properties of the Steenrod operations
Sq^{r}
and the Cartan formula. For details, the readers can see Steenrod and Epstein
[
27
]
.

Let
GL_{k}=GL_{k}(\mathbb{F}_{2})
be the general linear group over the field
\mathbb{F}_{2}
. This group acts naturally on
P_{k}
by matrix substitution. Since the two actions of
\mathcal{A}
and
GL_{k}
upon
P_{k}
commute with each other, there is an action of
GL_{k}
on
QP_{k}:=\mathbb{F}_{2}{\otimes}_{\mathcal{A}}P_{k}
.

For a nonnegative integer
n
, denote by
(P_{k})_{n}
the subspace of
P_{k}
consisting of all degree
n
homogeneous polynomials and by
(QP_{k})_{n}
the subspace of
QP_{k}
consisting of all the classes represented by homogeneous polynomials of degree
n
in
P_{k}
. Then
(P_{k})_{n}
and
(QP_{k})_{n}
are
GL_{k}
-submodules of
P_{k}
and
QP_{k}
respectively.

In 1989, Singer
[
25
]
defined the homological algebraic transfer

\varphi_{k}:\mbox{Tor}^{\mathcal{A}}_{k,n+k}(\mathbb{F}_{2},\mathbb{F}_{2})\longrightarrow(QP_{k})_{n}^{GL_{k}},

where
(QP_{k})_{n}^{GL_{k}}
denote the subspace of
(QP_{k})_{n}
consisting of all the
GL_{k}
-invariant classes in
(QP_{k})_{n}
and
\mbox{Tor}^{\mathcal{A}}_{k,k+n}(\mathbb{F}_{2},\mathbb{F}_{2})
is the dual of Ext
{}_{\mathcal{A}}^{k,k+n}(\mathbb{F}_{2},\mathbb{F}_{2})
, the
E_{2}
term of the Adams spectral sequence of spheres.

The algebraic transfer was studied by Boardman
[
2
]
, Bruner-Hà-Hưng
[
3
]
, Hà
[
7
]
, Hưng
[
8
,
9
,
10
]
, Chơn-Hà
[
5
,
6
]
, Hưng-Quỳnh
[
11
]
, Minami
[
14
]
, Nam
[
16
]
, Phúc
[
17
,
18
,
20
]
, Quỳnh
[
24
]
, and others.

Singer showed in
[
25
]
that
\varphi_{k}
is an isomorphism for
k=1,\,2
. Boardman showed in
[
2
]
that
\varphi_{3}
is also an isomorphism. However, Singer showed in
[
25
]
that
\varphi_{5}
is not a mononorphism in the degree 9. Then, Hưng proved in
[
10
]
that for any
k\geqslant 4
,
\varphi_{k}
is not a mononorphism in infinitely many degrees. Singer made the following conjecture.

Conjecture 1.1

(Singer
[
25
]
)
.

The algebraic transfer
\varphi_{k}
is an epimorphism for any
k\geqslant 0
.

Recently, Phúc stated in
[
17
]
that the conjecture is also true for
k=4
but the proof is not explicit and the computations are false and incomplete.

The purpose of the paper is to give a negative answer to this conjecture. The following is the main result of the paper.

Theorem 1.2
.

Singer’s conjecture is not true for
k=5
.

We prove this theorem by studying the Peterson hit problem of determining a minimal set of generators for the polynomial algebra
P_{5}
as a module over the Steenrod algebra. More precisely, we explicitly determine a set of generators for the
\mathbb{F}_{2}
-vector space
(QP_{5})_{108}
. Using this result we prove the following.

Theorem 1.3
.

There is a nonzero class
[p]
in the
\mathbb{F}_{2}
-vector space
(QP_{5})_{108}^{GL_{5}}
such that
[p]\in\langle[p_{j}]:1\leqslant j\leqslant 14\rangle
. Here, the polynomials
p_{j}
,
1\leqslant j\leqslant 14
, are defined as in Subsection
5.2
.

From the results in Lin
[
13
]
and Chen
[
4
]
, we have
\mbox{Ext}^{5,113}_{\mathcal{A}}(\mathbb{F}_{2},\mathbb{F}_{2})=0
. By passing to the dual, one gets
\mbox{Tor}_{5,113}^{\mathcal{A}}(\mathbb{F}_{2},\mathbb{F}_{2})=0
. Hence, Theorem
1.3
implies that the homomorphism

\varphi_{5}:\mbox{Tor}_{5,113}^{\mathcal{A}}(\mathbb{F}_{2},\mathbb{F}_{2})\to(QP_{5})_{108}^{GL_{5}}

is not an epimorphism. Theorem
1.2
is proved.

In
[
34
, Theorem 2.10]
, by using a computer program based on an algorithm of the mathematics system SAGEMATH, Tín proved a dimensional result that
\dim(QP_{5})_{108}=2171
. This result is also confirmed in Phúc
[
20
]
by using a computer program based on an algorithm of the MAGMA. However, there are no any details of computations for a basis of
(QP_{5})_{108}
.

To prove Theorem
1.3
, we explicitly determine a set of generators for
(QP_{5})_{108}
. We prove the following.

Theorem 1.4
.

There exist a set of generators for
(QP_{5})_{108}
consisting of all the classes represented by 2184 monomials which are determined as in Section
5.1
.

Since the basis of
(QP_{5})_{108}
has not yet been determined, we have been unable to explicitly determine the space
(QP_{5})_{108}^{GL_{5}}
. We prove the existence of the polynomial
p
by studying the
\mathbb{F}_{2}
-vector spaces
(\widetilde{QP}_{5})_{n}
and
(\widetilde{SF}_{k})_{n}
as defined below Lemma
2.2.1
. By using Theorem
1.4
we prove the following.

Theorem 1.5
.

The space
(\widetilde{SF}_{5})_{108}=\langle[p_{j}]:1\leqslant j\leqslant 14\rangle
is an
GL_{5}
-submodule of
(QP_{5})_{108}
and
\dim(\widetilde{QP}_{5})_{108}=2170.

Since
(QP_{5})_{108}\cong(\widetilde{QP}_{5})_{108}\bigoplus(\widetilde{SF}_{5})_{108}
and
\dim({QP}_{5})_{108}=2171,
from Theorem
1.5
we obtain
\dim(\widetilde{SF}_{5})_{108}=1
. Hence,
(\widetilde{SF}_{5})_{108}=\langle[p]\rangle
with
p
a nonzero class in
(QP_{5})_{108}
. By Theorem
1.5
,
(\widetilde{SF}_{5})_{108}
is an
GL_{5}
-module, hence
[p]
is an
GL_{5}
-invariant. Theorem
1.3
is proved.

Remark 1.6
.

After the initial manuscript was posted, we discovered several errors in Lemma 3.2.5 that seriously affected the main results. Consequently, that manuscript was not submitted to any journal.

We also note that the results of the paper depend on
\dim({QP}_{5})_{108}=2171
which was established by using the MAGMA and SAGEMATH computer programs. If it is correct, then there exists a unique index
j
,
1\leqslant j\leqslant 14
, such that the monomials
a_{1456+j}
in Subsection
5.1
is admissible and
[p]=[p_{j}].
If
\dim({QP}_{5})_{108}\neq 2171,
then the result of this paper is no longer valid. Then, all results obtained by using the MAGMA and SAGEMATH computer programs are unreliable.

Consider
n_{d}=2^{d+3}+2^{d+2}-4
with
d
a positive integer. Based on the results in
[
32
]
, we have showed that
\dim(QP_{5})_{n_{2}}^{GL_{5}}=1
. Based on the result
\dim({QP}_{5})_{n_{3}}=1706
which is confirmed in
[
20
]
and
[
34
]
by using the MAGMA and SAGEMATH computer programs we showed that
(\widetilde{SF}_{5})_{n_{3}}=\langle[\tilde{p}]\rangle
with
[\tilde{p}]
a nonzero class in
(QP_{5})_{n_{3}}^{GL_{5}}
. It is easy to see that
(\widetilde{SF}_{5})_{n_{d}}=0
for
d\geqslant 4
. We predict that the element
h_{0}g_{d}
is not in the image of
\varphi_{5}^{*}
for
d\geqslant 2
. Here,
g_{d}\in\mbox{Ext}^{4,n_{d}+4}_{\mathcal{A}}(\mathbb{F}_{2},\mathbb{F}_{2})
. This implies the following.

Conjecture 1.7
.

Singer conjecture for the fifth algebraic transfer is not true in the internal degrees
n_{2}=44
and
n_{3}=92
and it is true in the degree
n_{d}
for
d\geqslant 4
.

The proofs of Theorems
1.4
and
1.5
are extremely complicated. We prove them by combining hand computation with the aids of the Microsoft Excel software.

The paper is organized as follows. In Section
2
, we recall some definitions and results on the admissible monomials in
P_{k}
, Singer’s criterion on the hit monomials and establish some needed notations. Theorem
1.4
is proved in Section
3
by explicitly determining a system of
\mathcal{A}
-generators for
P_{5}
in degree
108
. The proof of Theorem
1.5
is presented in Section
4
. A set of generators for
(QP_{5})_{108}
is explicitly presented in Section
5
.

2.
Background on the Peterson hit problem

The purpose of the section is to present some needed definitions and results on the weight vector of a monomial, admissible monomial, Singer’s criterion for hit monomial from the work of Kameko
[
12
]
, Singer
[
26
]
and the present author
[
29
,
31
]
.

2.1.
The weight vector and the admissible monomials

Definition 2.1.1
.

Let
w=x_{1}^{b_{1}}x_{2}^{b_{2}}\ldots x_{k}^{b_{k}}
be a monomial in
P_{k}
. Denote
\nu_{j}(w)=b_{j},1\leqslant j\leqslant k
. We define

\displaystyle\omega(w)

\displaystyle=(\omega_{1}(w),\omega_{2}(w),\ldots,\omega_{i}(w),\ldots),\ \ \sigma(x)=(\nu_{1}(w),\nu_{2}(w),\ldots,\nu_{k}(w)),

where

\omega_{i}(w)=\sum_{1\leqslant j\leqslant k}\alpha_{i-1}(\nu_{j}(w)),\ i\geqslant 1.

The sequence
\omega(w)
is called the weight vector and
\sigma(w)
is called the exponent vectors of
w
.

Let
\omega=(\omega_{1},\omega_{2},\ldots,\omega_{i},\ldots)
be sequence of non-negative integers. The sequence
\omega
is called a weight vector if
\omega_{i}=0
for
i\gg 0
. We define
\deg\omega=\sum_{i>0}2^{i-1}\omega_{i}
, the length
\ell(\omega)=\max\{j:\omega_{j}>0\}
. Write
\omega=(\omega_{1},\omega_{2},\ldots,\omega_{r})
if
\omega_{j}=0
for
j>r
.

For weight vectors
\omega^{(1)}=(\omega_{1}^{(1)},\omega_{2}^{(1)},\ldots)
and
\omega^{(2)}=(\omega_{1}^{(2)},\omega_{2}^{(2)},\ldots)
, we define the concatenation of weight vectors
\omega^{(1)}|\omega^{(2)}=(\omega_{1}^{(1)},\ldots,\omega_{r}^{(1)},\omega_{1}^{(2)},\omega_{2}^{(2)},\ldots)
if
\ell(\omega^{(1)})=r
and
(a)|^{s}=(a)|(a)|\ldots|(a)
, (
s
times of
(a)
’s), where
a,\,s
are positive integers.

The sets of weight vectors and exponent vectors respectively are equipped with the left lexicographical order.

Denote by
P_{k}(\omega)=\langle u\in P_{k}:\deg u=\deg\omega,\mbox{ and }\omega(u)\leqslant\omega\rangle
and
P_{k}^{-}(\omega)=\langle u\in P_{k}(\omega):\omega(u)<\omega\rangle
.

Definition 2.1.2
.

Suppose
\omega
is a weight vector and
f,\,h
are two polynomials of the same degree in
P_{k}
. We define

i)
f\equiv h
if
f+h\in\mathcal{A}^{+}P_{k}
and
f
is called hit if
f\equiv 0
.

ii)
f\equiv_{\omega}h
if
f+h\in\mathcal{A}^{+}P_{k}+P_{k}^{-}(\omega)
.

Observe that
\equiv
and
\equiv_{\omega}
are equivalence relations. Set

QP_{k}(\omega)=P_{k}(\omega)/((\mathcal{A}^{+}P_{k}\cap P_{k}(\omega))+P_{k}^{-}(\omega)).

Proposition 2.1.3

(
[
30
]
)
.

If
\omega
is a weight vector, then the space
QP_{k}(\omega)
is an
GL_{k}
-module.

Definition 2.1.4
.

For
a,\,b
monomials in
P_{k}
with
\deg a=\deg b
, we define
a<b
if one of the following conditions satisfies:

i)
\omega(a)<\omega(b)
;

ii)
\omega(a)=\omega(b)
and
\sigma(a)<\sigma(b).

Definition 2.1.5
.

Let
w
be a monomial in
P_{k}
. The monomial
w
is said to be inadmissible if there are monomials
u_{1},u_{2},\ldots,u_{s}
such that
u_{j}<w
for
j=1,2,\ldots,s
and
w+\sum_{j=1}^{s}u_{j}\in\mathcal{A}^{+}P_{k}.

If
w
is not inadmissible, then it is called admissible.

We can see that, all the admissible monomials of degree
m
in
P_{k}
form a minimal set of
\mathcal{A}
-generators for
P_{k}
in degree
m
.

We denote
\mathcal{A}_{h}
the sub-Hopf algebra of
\mathcal{A}
generated by the Steenrod squares
Sq^{r}
with
0\leqslant r<2^{h}
, and
\mathcal{A}_{h}^{+}=\mathcal{A}^{+}\cap\mathcal{A}_{h}
.

Definition 2.1.6
.

Let
w
be a monomial
x
in
P_{k}
. The monomial
w
is said to be strictly inadmissible if there are monomials
u_{1},u_{2},\ldots,u_{s}
such that
u_{j}<x,
for
j=1,2,\ldots,s
and
w+\sum_{j=1}^{s}u_{j}\in\mathcal{A}_{r}^{+}P_{k}
with
r=\max\{j:\omega_{j}(w)>0\}
.

We recall the following in Kameko
[
12
]
and our work
[
28
]
].

Theorem 2.1.7
.

Let
u,v,w
be monomials in
P_{k}
such that
\omega_{i}(u)=0
for
i>t>0
,
\omega_{i}(w)=0
for
i>s>0
. Then we have

i)
If
w
is inadmissible, then the monomial
uw^{2^{t}}
is also inadmissible.

ii)
If
w
is strictly inadmissible, then so is the monomial
wv^{2^{s}}
.

We need a result of Singer
[
26
]
on the hit monomials in
P_{k}
.

Definition 2.1.8
.

Let
z
be a monomial in
P_{k}
. If
\nu_{j}(z)=2^{t_{u}}-1
with
t_{u}
a non-negative integer for
u=1,2,\ldots,k
, then
z
is called a spike. It is called the minimal spike if
t_{1}>t_{2}>\ldots>t_{h-1}\geqslant t_{h}>0
and
t_{j}=0
for
j>h
.

Theorem 2.1.9

(Singer
[
26
]
)
.

Let
f
be a monomial of degree
d
in
P_{k}
with
\mu(d)\leqslant k
and let
z
be the minimal spike of degree
d
. If
\omega(f)<\omega(z)
, then the monomial
f
is hit.

2.2.
Some other results and notations

We set

P_{k}^{0}=\langle\{u\in P_{k}:\nu_{t}(u)=0\mbox{ for some }t\}\rangle
and

P_{k}^{+}=\langle\{u\in P_{k}:\nu_{t}(u)>0,\mbox{ for all }t\}\rangle.
We see that
P_{k}^{0}
and
P_{k}^{+}
are the
\mathcal{A}
-submodules of
P_{k}
and
QP_{k}=QP_{k}^{0}\oplus QP_{k}^{+}.
Here, we denote
QP_{k}^{0}=P_{k}^{0}/\mathcal{A}^{+}P_{k}^{0}
and
QP_{k}^{+}=P_{k}^{+}/\mathcal{A}^{+}P_{k}^{+}
.

Set

\mathcal{N}_{k}=\{(j;J):J=(h_{1},h_{2},\ldots,h_{s}),1\leqslant j<h_{1}<\ldots<h_{s}\leqslant k,\ 0\leqslant s<k\}.

For
(j;J)\in\mathcal{N}_{k}
, we define the homomorphism of
\mathcal{A}
-algebras
p_{(j;J)}:P_{k}\to P_{k-1}
by substituting

p_{(j;J)}(x_{t})=\begin{cases}x_{t},&\mbox{ if }1\leqslant t<j,\\
\sum_{u\in I}x_{u-1},&\mbox{ if }t=j,\\
x_{t-1},&\mbox{ if }j<t\leqslant k.\end{cases}

(2.1)

Lemma 2.2.1

(
[
22
]
)
.

For any monomial
u
in
P_{k}
,
p_{(j;J)}(u)\in P_{k-1}(\omega(u)).

By Lemma
2.2.1
, if
u\in P_{k}(\omega)
, then
p_{(j;J)}(u)\in P_{k-1}(\omega)
for any weight vector
\omega
. Hence, we get the homomorphisms

\displaystyle p_{(j;J)}^{(\omega)}:QP_{k}(\omega)\longrightarrow QP_{k-1}(\omega),\ \ p_{(j;J)}^{(d)}:(QP_{k})_{m}\longrightarrow(QP_{k-1})_{d},

where
d=\deg\omega
. We denote

\displaystyle\widetilde{SF}_{k}(\omega)=\bigcap_{(j;J)\in\mathcal{N}_{k}}\mbox{Ker}(p_{(j;J)}^{(\omega)})\mbox{ and }(\widetilde{SF}_{k})_{d}=\bigcap_{(j;J)\in\mathcal{N}_{k}}\mbox{Ker}(p_{(j;J)}^{(d)}),

\displaystyle\widetilde{QP}_{k}(\omega)=QP_{k}(\omega)/\widetilde{SF}_{k}(\omega)\mbox{ and }(\widetilde{QP}_{k})_{d}=(QP_{k})_{d}/(\widetilde{SF}_{k})_{d},

It is easy to see that
\widetilde{SF}_{k}(\omega)
and
(\widetilde{SF}_{k})_{d}
are the subspaces of
QP_{k}^{+}(\omega)
and
(QP_{k}^{+})_{d}
respectively.

From the results in Kameko
[
12
]
and our work
[
29
]
, we see that for
k\leqslant 4
, the spaces
\widetilde{SF}_{k}(\omega)
and
(\widetilde{SF}_{k})_{d}
are the
GL_{k}
-submodules of
QP_{k}(\omega)
and
(QP_{k})_{d}
respectively. Based on this result and the ones in Adams, Gunawardena and Miller
[
1
]
, one can give the following prediction.

Conjecture 2.2.2
.

For any weight vector
\omega
of degree
d
, the spaces
\widetilde{SF}_{k}(\omega)
and
(\widetilde{SF}_{k})_{d}
are the
GL_{k}
-submodules of
QP_{k}(\omega)
and
(QP_{k})_{d}
respectively.

Notation 2.2.3
.

For any
h\in P_{k}
, we denote
[h]
the class in
QP_{k}
represented by
h
. If
h\in P_{k}(\omega)
, we denote
[h]_{\omega}
the class in
QP_{k}(\omega)
represented by
h
.
For a subset
L
of
P_{k}
, we denote
[L]=\{[h]:h\in L\}
. If
L\subset P_{k}(\omega)
, then we set
[L]_{\omega}=\{[h]_{\omega}:h\in L\}
. Denote by
|L|
the cardinal of
L
.

Let
d
be a nonnegative integer. Denote by
B_{k}(d)
the set of all admissible monomials of degree
d
in
P_{k}
. Set

B_{k}^{0}(d)=B_{k}(d)\cap P_{k}^{0},\ B_{k}^{+}(d)=B_{k}(d)\cap P_{k}^{+}.

For any weight vector
\omega
of degree
d
, we denote

B_{k}(\omega)=B_{k}(d)\cap P_{k}(\omega),\ B_{k}^{+}(\omega)=B_{k}^{+}(d)\cap P_{k}(\omega),\ QP_{k}^{+}(\omega):=QP_{k}(\omega)\cap QP_{k}^{+}.

For
I=(s_{1},s_{2},\ldots,s_{r}),\,1\leqslant s_{1}<\ldots<s_{r}\leqslant k
, we define a monomorphism
\theta_{I}:P_{r}\to P_{k}
of
\mathcal{A}
-algebras by substituting

\theta_{I}(x_{v})=x_{s_{v}}\ \mbox{ for }\ 1\leqslant v\leqslant r.

(2.2)

Clearly, for an arbitrary weight vector
\omega
of degree
d
,

Q\theta_{I}(P_{r}^{+})(\omega)\cong QP_{r}^{+}(\omega)\mbox{ and }(Q\theta_{I}(P_{r}^{+}))_{d}\cong(QP_{r}^{+})_{d}

for
1\leqslant r\leqslant k
. Here,
Q\theta_{I}(P_{r}^{+})=\theta_{I}(P_{r}^{+})/\mathcal{A}^{+}\theta_{I}(P_{r}^{+})
. Then we have

B_{k}(\omega)=\bigcup_{\mu(d)\leqslant r\leqslant k,\atop\ell(I)=r}\theta_{I}(B_{r}^{+}(\omega)),\ B_{k}(d)=\bigcup_{\mu(d)\leqslant r\leqslant k,\atop\ell(I)=r}\theta_{I}(B_{r}^{+}(d))

Set
J_{r}=(1,\ldots,\hat{r},\ldots,k)
for
1\leqslant r\leqslant k
.

Proposition 2.2.4

(Mothebe-Uys
[
15
]
)
.

Suppose
r,\,h
are integers such that
1\leqslant r\leqslant k
. If
f\in B_{k-1}(d)
, then
x_{r}^{2^{h}-1}\theta_{J_{r}}(f)\in B_{k}(d+2^{h}-1)
.

2.3.
A construction for the generators of
P_{k}

Definition 2.3.1

(
[
29
]
)
.

We set
x_{(I,r)}=x_{i_{r}}^{2^{s-1}+\ldots+2^{s-r}}\prod_{r<t\leqslant s}x_{i_{t}}^{2^{s-t}}
for any
(j;J)\in\mathcal{N}_{k}
with
J=(i_{1},\ldots,i_{s}),\ s>0
.
For a monomial
w\in P_{k-1}
, we define
\phi_{(j;J)}(w)\in P_{k}
by setting

\phi_{(j;J)}(w)=\begin{cases}\theta_{J_{j}}(w),&\mbox{if }s=0,\\
(x_{j}^{2^{r}-1}\theta_{J_{j}}(w))/x_{(J,r)},&\mbox{if there is $1\leqslant r\leqslant s$ such that }\\
&\quad\nu_{i_{1}-1}(w)=\ldots=\nu_{i_{(r-1)}-1}(w)=2^{s}-1,\\
&\quad\nu_{i_{r}-1}(w)>2^{s}-1,\\
&\quad\alpha_{s-t}(\nu_{i_{r}-1}(w))=1,\ \forall t,\ 1\leqslant t\leqslant r,\\
&\quad\alpha_{s-t}(\nu_{i_{t}-1}(w))=1,\ \forall t,\ r<t\leqslant r,\\
0,&\mbox{otherwise.}\end{cases}

For a subset
B\subset P_{k}
, we denote

\displaystyle\Phi^{0}(B)=\bigcup_{1\leqslant j\leqslant k}\phi_{(j;\emptyset)}(B),\ \ \Phi^{+}(B)=\bigcup_{(j;J)\in\mathcal{N}_{k},0<\ell(J)\leqslant k-1}\phi_{(j;J)}(B)\setminus P_{n}^{0},

and
\Phi(B)=\Phi^{0}(B)\bigcup\Phi^{+}(B)
.
It is easy to see that for any minimal set of generators
B
of
\mathcal{A}
-module
P_{k-1}
in degree
d
,
\Phi^{0}(B)
is a minimal set of generators for
\mathcal{A}
-module
P_{k}^{0}
in degree
d
and
\Phi^{+}(B)\subset P_{k}^{+}
.

Theorem 2.3.2

(See
[
29
,
33
]
)
.

Let
n=(k-1)(2^{d}-1)+q.2^{d}
with
d,q
positive integers such that
1\leqslant k-3\leqslant\mu(q)\leqslant k-2
,
\alpha(q+\mu(q))=\mu(q)
. If
d\geqslant k-1
, then
B_{k}(n)=\Phi(B_{k-1}(n))
.

In
[
29
, Pages 445-446]
we have proved that from Theorem
2.3.2
we get

\dim(QP_{k})_{n}=(2^{k}-1)\dim(QP_{k-1})_{n}.

Then we set
h_{u}=(k-1)(2^{d-u}-1)+q.2^{d-u}
for
0\leqslant u\leqslant d
,
h_{0}=n
,
h_{d}=q
. By using a result in Kameko
[
12
, Theorem 1.1]
, we get

\displaystyle(QP_{k-1})_{h_{u}}\cong(QP_{k-1})_{h_{u-1}},\ 1\leqslant u\leqslant d.

(2.3)

This means that
\dim(QP_{k})_{n}=(2^{k}-1)\dim(QP_{k-1})_{h_{u}}
for
1\leqslant u\leqslant d
.
In particular, we have

Theorem 2.3.3

(See
[
29
]
)
.

Let
n=(k-1)(2^{d}-1)+q.2^{d}
with
d,q
positive integers such that
1\leqslant k-3\leqslant\mu(q)\leqslant k-2
and
\alpha(q+\mu(q))=\mu(q)
. If
d\geqslant k-1
, then

\dim(QP_{k})_{n}=(2^{k}-1)\dim(QP_{k-1})_{q}.

By combining Theorem
2.3.2
and
2.3.3
we easily obtain the following.

Corollary 2.3.4
.

Let
\omega
be a weight vector. If
d\geqslant k-1
, then
B_{k}((k-1)|^{d}|\omega)=\Phi(B_{k-1}((k-1)|^{d}|\omega))
and
\dim QP_{k}((k-1)|^{d}|\omega)=(2^{k}-1)\dim QP_{k-1}(\omega)
.

Remark 2.3.5
.

Phúc presents a formula in
[
20
]
as follows. Let
n_{s}:=(k-1)(2^{s}-1)+q.2^{s}
. If there is a non-negative integer
\zeta
such that
\zeta<s
and
1\leqslant k-3\leqslant\mu(n_{\zeta})=\alpha(n_{\zeta}+\mu(n_{\zeta}))\leqslant k-2
, then with the generic degrees of the form
(k-1)(2^{s-\zeta+k-1}-1)+n_{\zeta}.2^{s-\zeta+k-1}
, where
q=n_{\zeta}
and
s\geqslant\zeta\geqslant 0
,
he has shown in
[
18
]
that for every
s\geqslant\zeta
,

\dim(QP_{k})_{(k-1)(2^{s-\zeta+k-1}-1)+n_{\zeta}.2^{s-\zeta+k-1}}=(2^{k}-1)\dim(QP_{k-1})_{n_{s}}.

(2.4)

However, with
q=n_{\zeta}=(k-1)(2^{\zeta}-1)+q.2^{\zeta}
, we get
\zeta=0
. Then,
n=(k-1)(2^{s-\zeta+k-1}-1)+n_{\zeta}.2^{s-\zeta+q-1}=(k-1)(2^{s+k-1}-1)+q.2^{s+k-1}
.
Thus, the author of
[
20
]
obtains the formula (
2.4
) by plagiarizing from the proof of Theorem
2.3.3
in
[
29
, Pages 445-446]
with
d=s+k-1
and
n_{s}=h_{k-1}
.

For the case
\zeta\geqslant 0
, in
[
18
]
, the author write
n_{s}:=(k-1)(2^{s}-1)+r.2^{s}
, so
n_{\zeta}=(k-1)(2^{\zeta}-1)+r.2^{\zeta}
. Hence,
n_{s}=(k-1)(2^{s-\zeta}-1)+n_{\zeta}.2^{s-\zeta}
. Thus, the formula (
2.4
) in Phúc
[
18
, Page 15]
is also plagiarized from the proof of Theorem
2.3.3
in
[
29
]
with
q=n_{\zeta}
,
d=s-\zeta+k-1
and
n_{s}=h_{k-1}
. So, Phúc’s explanations in
[
21
, Appendix]
are false. Kameko’s isomorphism is used to prove (
2.3
). Therefore, there are no any new contributions of the author
[
18
]
for the formula (
2.4
). Recently, the correctness of the above comments are confirmed in
[
19
]
.

3.
Proof of Theorem
1.4

In this sction, we explicitly determine all admissible monomials of the degree 108 in
P_{5}
.

3.1.
The weight vector of admissible monomials in the space
(P_{5})_{108}

First of all, we determine the weight vectors of the admissible monomials of the degree 108 in
P_{5}
.

Lemma 3.1.1
.

If
x
is an admissible monomial of degree
n=2^{d+3}+2^{d+2}+2^{d+1}-4
in
P_{5}
with an integer
d\geqslant 2
, then

\omega(x)=(4)|^{d}|(2)|^{2}|(1)\mbox{ or }\omega(x)=(4)|^{d+1}|(1)|^{2}\mbox{ or }\omega(x)=(4)|^{d+1}|(3).

The result of the lemma is stated in
[
20
]
but there are no any details of the proof. We need a technical result for the proof of this lemma.

Lemma 3.1.2
.

Let
(i,j,t,u,v)
be a permutation of
(1,2,3,4,5)
. The following monomials are strictly inadmissible:

i)

x_{i}^{2}x_{j}x_{t}^{3}x_{u}^{3}x_{v}^{3},\,x_{i}^{3}x_{j}^{4}x_{t}^{7}x_{u}^{7}x_{v}^{7},\,i<j.

ii)

x_{i}^{7}x_{j}^{8}x_{t}^{11}x_{u}^{11}x_{v}^{15}
,
x_{i}^{7}x_{j}^{9}x_{t}^{10}x_{u}^{11}x_{v}^{15}
,
x_{i}^{7}x_{j}^{9}x_{t}^{11}x_{u}^{11}x_{v}^{14}
,
x_{i}^{7}x_{j}^{11}x_{t}^{11}x_{u}^{13}x_{v}^{10}
,

x_{i}^{7}x_{j}^{11}x_{t}^{11}x_{u}^{11}x_{v}^{12}
.

Proof.

Part i) is proved in
[
23
]
. Note that all monomials in Part ii) are of weight vector
(4,4,2,4)
. We prove Part ii) for
x=x_{1}^{7}x_{2}^{11}x_{3}^{11}x_{4}^{11}x_{5}^{12}
. The computations for others are easy. By using the Cartan formula, we have

\displaystyle x

\displaystyle=Sq^{1}\big(x_{i}^{7}x_{j}^{7}x_{t}^{7}x_{u}^{11}x_{v}^{19}+x_{i}^{7}x_{j}^{7}x_{t}^{7}x_{u}^{19}x_{v}^{11}+x_{i}^{7}x_{j}^{11}x_{t}^{11}x_{u}^{9}x_{v}^{13}\big)+Sq^{2}\big(x_{i}^{7}x_{j}^{7}x_{t}^{7}x_{u}^{10}x_{v}^{19}

\displaystyle\quad+x_{i}^{7}x_{j}^{7}x_{t}^{7}x_{u}^{18}x_{v}^{11}+x_{i}^{7}x_{j}^{7}x_{t}^{11}x_{u}^{3}x_{v}^{22}+x_{i}^{7}x_{j}^{7}x_{t}^{19}x_{u}^{3}x_{v}^{14}\big)+Sq^{4}\big(x_{i}^{11}x_{j}^{7}x_{t}^{7}x_{u}^{11}x_{v}^{12}

\displaystyle\quad+x_{i}^{11}x_{j}^{7}x_{t}^{7}x_{u}^{10}x_{v}^{13}+x_{i}^{11}x_{j}^{7}x_{t}^{11}x_{u}^{5}x_{v}^{14}+x_{i}^{11}x_{j}^{7}x_{t}^{13}x_{u}^{3}x_{v}^{14}\big)+Sq^{8}\big(x_{i}^{7}x_{j}^{7}x_{t}^{7}x_{u}^{11}x_{v}^{12}

\displaystyle\quad+x_{i}^{7}x_{j}^{7}x_{t}^{7}x_{u}^{10}x_{v}^{13}+x_{i}^{7}x_{j}^{7}x_{t}^{11}x_{u}^{5}x_{v}^{14}+x_{i}^{7}x_{j}^{7}x_{t}^{13}x_{u}^{3}x_{v}^{14}\big)\ \mbox{ mod}(P_{5}^{-}(4,4,2,4)).

The above equality shows that the monomial
x
is strictly inadmissible.
∎

Proof of Lemma
3.1.1
.

Let
x
be an admissible monomial of degree
n=2^{d+3}+2^{d+2}+2^{d+1}-4
in
P_{5}
with
d
a positive integer. By Lemma 2.14 in
[
28
]
,
\omega_{i}(x)=4
for
1\leqslant i\leqslant d
. Hence,
x=\bar{x}y^{2^{d}}
with
\bar{x}
a monomial of weight vector
(4)|^{d}
and
y
a monomial of degree 10 in
P_{5}
. By a result in Tín
[
35
]
,
\omega(y)
is one of the weight vectors
(2,2,1)
,
(2,4)
,
(4,1,1)
,
(4,3)
. If
d\geqslant 2
and
\omega(y)=(2,4)
, then either there is a monomial
w
as given in Lemma
3.1.2
(i) such that
x=y_{1}w^{2^{r}}y_{2}^{2^{d-r-s}}
with
y_{1}
a monomial of weight vector
(4)|^{r}
and
y_{2}
a monomial of weight vector
(4)|^{d-r-s}|(2)|(4)
,
2\leqslant s\leqslant 3
,
r+s\leqslant d
, or there is a monomial
u
as given in Lemma
3.1.2
(ii) such that
x=y_{3}u^{d-2}
with
y_{3}
a monomial of weight vector
(4)|^{d-2}
. By Theorem
2.1.7
,
x
is inadmissible. Hence, the lemma is proved.
∎

By applying Lemma
3.1.1
with
d=3
, we obtain the following.

Corollary 3.1.3
.

We have

(QP_{5})_{108}\cong QP_{5}((4)|^{3}|(2)|^{2}|(1))\bigoplus QP_{5}((4)|^{4}|(1)|^{2})\bigoplus QP_{5}((4)|^{4}|(3)).

According to
[
29
]
,

\displaystyle\dim QP_{4}((4)|^{4}|(1)|^{2})=\dim QP_{4}((1)|^{2})=10,

\displaystyle\dim QP_{4}((4)|^{4}|(3))=\dim QP_{4}((3))=4.

Hence, by using Corollary
2.3.4
we get

\dim QP_{5}((4)|^{4}|(1)|^{2})=31\times 10=310,\ \dim QP_{5}((4)|^{4}|(3))=31\times 4=124.

Thus, we need only to determined the space
QP_{5}((4)|^{3}|(2)|^{2}|(1))
.

3.2.
Computation of
QP_{5}((4)|^{3}|(2)|^{2}|(1))

We have

B_{5}((4)|^{3}|(2)|^{2}|(1))=B_{5}^{0}((4)|^{3}|(2)|^{2}|(1))\bigcup B_{5}^{+}((4)|^{3}|(2)|^{2}|(1)),

where
B_{5}^{0}((4)|^{3}|(2)|^{2}|(1))=\Phi^{0}(B_{4}((4)|^{3}|(2)|^{2}|(1))))
. By
[

]
,
B_{4}((4)|^{3}|(2)|^{2}|(1)))
is the set of
56
monomials

, which are determined as in Section
5
. We have
|B_{5}^{0}((4)|^{3}|(2)|^{2}|(1))|=5\times 56=280
.

We set
\mathcal{B}:=\left\{x_{i}^{63}\theta_{J_{i}}(x):x\in B_{4}^{+}((3)|^{3}|(1)|^{2}),1\leqslant i\leqslant 5\right\}.

According to
[
29
]
, we have
|B_{4}^{+}((3)|^{3}|(1)|^{2})|=66

2.2.4

is the set of 330 monomials determined as in Section

and
\mathcal{B}\subset B_{5}^{+}((4)|^{3}|(2)|^{2}|(1))
. A direct computation shows that
\Phi^{+}(B_{4}((4)|^{3}|(2)|^{2}|(1)))
is the set of 1403 monomials.

In this subsection we prove the following.

Lemma 3.2.2
.

Let
1\leqslant i\leqslant 5
and the homomorphism
\theta_{J_{i}}:P_{4}\to P_{5}
defined by (
2.2
) with
k=5
and
J_{i}=(1,\ldots,\hat{i},\ldots,5)
.

i)
The monomial
x_{i}^{15}\theta_{J_{i}}\big(x_{1}^{7}x_{2}^{7}x_{3}^{8}x_{4}^{7}\big)
is strictly inadmissible.

ii)
If
x
is one of the monomials
x_{1}^{7}x_{2}^{7}x_{3}^{15}x_{4}^{16}
,
x_{1}^{7}x_{2}^{15}x_{3}^{7}x_{4}^{16}
,
x_{1}^{7}x_{2}^{15}x_{3}^{16}x_{4}^{7}
,
x_{1}^{7}x_{2}^{15}x_{3}^{17}x_{4}^{6}
,
x_{1}^{15}x_{2}^{7}x_{3}^{7}x_{4}^{16}
,
x_{1}^{15}x_{2}^{7}x_{3}^{16}x_{4}^{7}
,
x_{1}^{15}x_{2}^{7}x_{3}^{17}x_{4}^{6}
,
x_{1}^{15}x_{2}^{19}x_{3}^{5}x_{4}^{6}
, then
x_{i}^{31}\theta_{J_{i}}(x)
is strictly inadmissible.

iii)
The following monomials are strictly inadmissible:

x_{1}^{7}x_{2}^{15}x_{3}^{16}x_{4}^{15}x_{5}^{23}

x_{1}^{7}x_{2}^{15}x_{3}^{17}x_{4}^{14}x_{5}^{23}

x_{1}^{7}x_{2}^{15}x_{3}^{17}x_{4}^{15}x_{5}^{22}

x_{1}^{7}x_{2}^{15}x_{3}^{17}x_{4}^{30}x_{5}^{7}

x_{1}^{15}x_{2}^{7}x_{3}^{16}x_{4}^{15}x_{5}^{23}

x_{1}^{15}x_{2}^{7}x_{3}^{17}x_{4}^{14}x_{5}^{23}

x_{1}^{15}x_{2}^{7}x_{3}^{17}x_{4}^{15}x_{5}^{22}

x_{1}^{15}x_{2}^{7}x_{3}^{17}x_{4}^{30}x_{5}^{7}

x_{1}^{15}x_{2}^{15}x_{3}^{16}x_{4}^{7}x_{5}^{23}

x_{1}^{15}x_{2}^{15}x_{3}^{16}x_{4}^{23}x_{5}^{7}

x_{1}^{15}x_{2}^{15}x_{3}^{23}x_{4}^{16}x_{5}^{7}

x_{1}^{15}x_{2}^{19}x_{3}^{5}x_{4}^{14}x_{5}^{23}

x_{1}^{15}x_{2}^{19}x_{3}^{5}x_{4}^{15}x_{5}^{22}

x_{1}^{15}x_{2}^{19}x_{3}^{5}x_{4}^{30}x_{5}^{7}

x_{1}^{15}x_{2}^{19}x_{3}^{15}x_{4}^{5}x_{5}^{22}

x_{1}^{15}x_{2}^{19}x_{3}^{15}x_{4}^{21}x_{5}^{6}

x_{1}^{15}x_{2}^{23}x_{3}^{7}x_{4}^{15}x_{5}^{16}

x_{1}^{15}x_{2}^{23}x_{3}^{15}x_{4}^{7}x_{5}^{16}

x_{1}^{15}x_{2}^{23}x_{3}^{15}x_{4}^{16}x_{5}^{7}

x_{1}^{15}x_{2}^{23}x_{3}^{15}x_{4}^{17}x_{5}^{6}.

Proof.

The proof of Parts i) and ii) are easy. We prove Part iii) for 6 monomials

W_{1}=x_{1}^{7}x_{2}^{15}x_{3}^{16}x_{4}^{15}x_{5}^{23}

W_{2}=x_{1}^{15}x_{2}^{7}x_{3}^{17}x_{4}^{15}x_{5}^{22}

W_{3}=x_{1}^{15}x_{2}^{15}x_{3}^{23}x_{4}^{16}x_{5}^{7}

The above equalities show that the monomials
W_{j},\,1\leqslant j\leqslant 6
, are strictly inadmissible. The proof for other monomials are carried out by a similar computation.

We prove the lemma for 8 monomials

Z_{8}=x_{1}^{15}x_{2}^{17}x_{3}^{7}x_{4}^{10}x_{5}^{3}
.

These equalities imply the monomials
Z_{j},\,1\leqslant j\leqslant 8
, are strictly inadmissible.
∎

Lemma 3.2.5
.

i)
If
(i,j,t,u,v)
is an arbitrary permutation of
(1,2,3,4,5)
, then the monomials
x_{i}^{7}x_{j}^{7}x_{t}^{31}x_{u}^{31}x_{v}^{32}
,
x_{i}^{7}x_{j}^{15}x_{t}^{23}x_{u}^{31}x_{v}^{32}
,
x_{i}^{15}x_{j}^{15}x_{t}^{23}x_{u}^{23}x_{v}^{32}
are strictly inadmissible except for monomials
x_{1}^{15}x_{2}^{15}x_{3}^{23}x_{4}^{23}x_{5}^{32}
and
x_{1}^{15}x_{2}^{15}x_{3}^{23}x_{4}^{32}x_{5}^{23}
.

ii)
The following monomials are strictly inadmissible:

x_{1}^{7}x_{2}^{15}x_{3}^{15}x_{4}^{55}x_{5}^{16}

∎

Proof of Proposition
3.2.1
.

By a simple computation we have

\Phi^{+}(B_{4}((4)|^{3}|(2)|^{2}|(1)))\cup\mathcal{C}=\{a_{s}:1\leqslant s\leqslant 1470\},

where the monomials
a_{s}
are listed as in the Subsection
5.1
.

Let
x
be an admissible monomial of weight vector
(4)|^{3}|(2)|^{2}|(1)
and
x\in P_{5}^{+}
. By a direct computation we see that if
x\notin\Phi^{+}(B_{4}((4)|^{3}|(2)|^{2}|(1)))\cup\mathcal{C}
, then either
x
is one of the monomials as listed in Lemma
3.2.5
or there is a monomials as listed in one of Lemmas
3.1.2
(i),
3.2.2
,
3.2.3
,
3.2.4
such that
x=yw^{2^{r}}z^{2^{r+u}}
with
1\leqslant r\leqslant 5
,
u\geqslant 0
,
r+u\leqslant 5
and
y
,
z
suitable monomials in
P_{5}
. By Theorem
2.1.7
,
x
is inadmissible and we have a contradiction. Hence,

B_{5}^{+}((4)|^{3}(2)|^{2}|(1))\subset\Phi^{+}(B_{4}((4)|^{3}|(2)|^{2}|(1)))\cup\mathcal{C}.

The proposition is proved.
∎

4.
Proof of Theorem
1.5

From the proof of Proposition 3.3 in
[
29
]
we get
\widetilde{SF}_{5}((4)|^{4}|(1)|^{2})=0
and
\widetilde{SF}_{5}((4)|^{4}|(3))=0
. Hence, we need only to determined
\widetilde{SF}_{5}((4)|^{3}|(2)|^{2}|(1))\subset QP_{5}^{+}((4)|^{3}|(2)|^{2}|(1)).

We can easily verify that
[p_{j}]\in\widetilde{SF}_{5}((4)|^{3}|(2)|^{2}|(1))
for
1\leqslant j\leqslant 14
. The leading monomial of
p_{j}
is
a_{1456+j}
.
Since
B_{5}^{+}((4)|^{3}|(2)|^{2}|(1))\subset\{[a_{t}]:1\leqslant t\leqslant 1470\}
, if
\xi\in\widetilde{SF}_{5}((4)|^{3}|(2)|^{2}|(1))
, then there are
\delta_{j}\in\mathbb{F}_{2}
,
1\leqslant j\leqslant 14
, such that

\theta=\xi+\sum_{1\leqslant j\leqslant 14}\delta_{j}p_{j}\equiv\sum_{1\leqslant t\leqslant 1456}\gamma_{t}a_{t},

(4.1)

where
\gamma_{t}\in\mathbb{F}_{2}
,
1\leqslant t\leqslant 1470
. Then, we have
\theta\in\widetilde{SF}_{5}((4)|^{3}|(2)|^{2}|(1))
and
p_{(i;I)}(\theta)\equiv 0
for all
(i;I)\in\mathcal{N}_{5}
.

By a direct computation from the relations
p_{(i;I)}(\theta)\equiv 0
for all
(i;I)\in\mathcal{N}_{5}
we obtain
\gamma_{t}=0
for
1\leqslant t\leqslant 1456
. Hence,
\theta\equiv 0
and
\xi\equiv\sum_{1\leqslant j\leqslant 14}\delta_{j}p_{j}
. This implies

(\widetilde{SF}_{5})_{108}=\widetilde{SF}_{5}((4)|^{3}|(2)|^{2}|(1))=\langle[p_{j}]:1\leqslant j\leqslant 14\rangle

and
\widetilde{QP}_{5}^{+}((4)|^{3}|(2)|^{2}|(1))\cong\langle[a_{t}]:1\leqslant t\leqslant 1456\rangle.
So, we get

\dim(\widetilde{QP}_{5})_{108}=124+310+280+1456=2170.

We can easily verify that
p_{(i;I)}(\rho_{u}(p_{j}))\equiv 0
for all
(i;I)\in\mathcal{N}_{5}
. Hence
\rho_{u}([p_{j}])\in(\widetilde{SF}_{5})_{108}
for
1\leqslant u\leqslant 5
and
1\leqslant j\leqslant 14
. This means that
(\widetilde{SF}_{5})_{108}
is an
GL_{5}
-submodule of
(QP_{5})_{108}
.
